Figure 0.A.2
:

An operational semantics for transactional programs. Above,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
denotes the last transaction log in the session order
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}(j)
of
h
while
\mathsf{snapshot}_{\iota}
and
\mathsf{readFrom}
denote the snapshot visible to an instruction and the writes it reads from, respectively. The
\mathsf{validate}_{\iota}{}
checks if a transaction can be committed. They are defined in Figure
0.A.3
.

.

Formally, the operational semantics is defined as a transition relation
\Rightarrow
between
configurations
. A configuration is a tuple containing the following:

•

history
{h}
recording the instructions executed in the past,

•

a valuation map
\vec{\gamma}
that records local variable values in the current transaction of each session (
\vec{\gamma}
associates identifiers of sessions that have live transactions with valuations of local variables),

•

a map
\vec{\mathsf{B}}
that stores the code of each live transaction (associating session identifiers with code),

•

a map
\vec{\mathsf{I}}
that tracks the isolation level of each executed transaction,

•

a map
\vec{\mathsf{T}}
that associates events in the history with unique timestamps,

•

a map
\vec{\mathsf{S}}
that associates events in the history with snapshots of the database,

•

sessions/transactions
\mathsf{P}
that remain to be executed from the original program.

For readability, we define a program as a partial function
\mathsf{P}:\mathsf{SessId}\rightharpoonup\mathsf{Sess}
that associates session identifiers in
\mathsf{SessId}
with sequences of transactions as defined in Section
2.1
. Similarly, the session order
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
in a history is defined as a partial function
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}:\mathsf{SessId}\rightharpoonup\mathsf{Tlogs}^{*}
that associates session identifiers with sequences of transaction logs. Two transaction logs are ordered by
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
if one occurs before the other in some sequence
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}(j)
with

j\in\mathsf{SessId}
.

Before presenting the definition of
\Rightarrow_{I}
, we introduce some notation. Let
{h}
be a history that contains a representation of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
as above. We use
{h}\oplus_{j}{({t,\iota_{t},E,{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}_{t}})}
to denote a history where
{({t,\iota_{t},E,{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}_{t}})}
is appended to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}(j)
.
Also, for an event
e
,
{h}\oplus_{j}e
is the history obtained from
{h}
by adding
e
to the last transaction log in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}(j)
and as a last event in the program order of this log (i.e., if
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}(j)=\sigma;{({t,\iota_{t},E,{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}_{t}})}
, then the session order
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}
of
{h}\oplus_{j}e
is defined by
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}(k)={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}(k)
for all
k\neq j
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}(j)=\sigma;{({t,\iota_{t},E\cup{e},{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}\cup\{(e^{\prime},e):e^{\prime}\in E\}})}
). Finally, for a history
{h}={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
,
{h}\oplus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}(t,e)
is the history obtained from
{h}
by adding
(t,e)
to the write-read relation.

Figures
0.A.1
and
0.A.2
list the rules defining
\Rightarrow
. We distinguish between local computation rules (
if-true
,
if-false
and
local
) and database-accesses rules (
begin
,
insert
,
select
,
update
,
delete
,
commit
and
abort
); each associated to its homonymous instruction. Database-accesses get an increasing timestamp
\tau
as well as an isolation-depending snapshot of the database using predicate
\mathsf{snapshot}_{\iota}
; updating adequately the timestamp and snapshot maps (
\vec{\mathsf{T}}
and
\vec{\mathsf{S}}
respectively).
Timestamps are used for validating the writes of a transaction and blocking inconsistent runs as well as for defining the set of possible snapshots any event can get. We use predicate
\mathsf{readFrom}
for determining the values read by an event. Those reads depend on both the event’s snapshot as well as the timestamp of every previously executed event. Their formal definitions are described in Figure
0.A.3
.

The
begin
rule starts a new transaction, provided that there is no other live transaction (
\mathsf{B}=\epsilon
) in the same session. It adds an empty transaction log to the history and schedules the body of the transaction.

if-true
and
if-false
check the truth value of a Boolean condition of an
\mathtt{if}
conditional.
local
handles the case where some local computation is required.
insert
,
select
,
update
and
delete
handle the database accesses.
insert
add some rows
\mathsf{R}
in the history.
select
,
update
and
delete
read every key from a combination of its snapshot and the local writes defined by
\mathsf{readFrom}
function. The predicate
{\_}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {\_}
implicitly uses the previous information stored in the history via the function
\mathsf{value}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}}
. Finally
commit
and
abort
validate that the run of the transaction correspond to the isolation level specification. These rules may block in case the validation is not satisfied as the predicate valuation does not change with the application of posterior rules.

An
initial
configuration for program
{\mathsf{P}}
contains the program
{\mathsf{P}}
along with a history

{h}={({\{t_{0}\},\emptyset,\emptyset})}
, where
t_{0}
is a transaction log containing only writes that write the initial values of all keys and whose timestamp and snapshot is
0
(
\vec{\mathsf{S}},\vec{\mathsf{T}}=[t_{0}\mapsto 0])
, and it does not contain transaction code nor local keys (
\vec{\gamma},\vec{\mathsf{B}}=\emptyset
).
A
run

\rho
of a program
{\mathsf{P}}

is a sequence of configurations
c_{0}c_{1}\ldots c_{n}
where
c_{0}
is an initial configuration for
{\mathsf{P}}
, and
c_{m}\Rightarrow c_{m+1}
, for every
0\leq m<n
. We say that
c_{n}
is
reachable
from
c_{0}
.
The history of such a run,
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
, is the history
{h}_{n}
in the last configuration
c_{n}
.
A configuration is called
final
if it contains the empty program (
{\mathsf{P}}=\emptyset
).
Let
{\mathsf{hist}({{\mathsf{P}}})}
denote the set of all histories of a run of
{\mathsf{P}}
that ends in a final configuration.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{snapshot}_{\textup{{SER}}}({h},\vec{\mathsf{S}},\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime},e,\xi)=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\max\left\{\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(c_{t^{\prime}})\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}t^{\prime}\in h\ \land\\
c_{t^{\prime}}=\mathtt{commit}(t^{\prime})\ \land\\[2.84526pt]
\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(c_{t^{\prime}})<\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(e)\end{array}\right.\right\}&\text{if }\xi=\mathtt{begin}\\[14.22636pt]
\vec{\mathsf{S}}(\mathtt{begin}(\mathsf{tr}(e)))&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.\\[22.76219pt]
\mathsf{snapshot}_{\textup{{SI}}}({h},\vec{\mathsf{S}},\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime},e,\xi)=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{choice}\left(\left\{\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(c_{t^{\prime}})\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}t^{\prime}\in h\ \land\\[2.84526pt]
c_{t^{\prime}}=\mathtt{commit}(t^{\prime})\ \land\\[2.84526pt]
vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(c_{t^{\prime}})<\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(e)\end{array}\right.\right\}\right)&\text{if }\xi=\mathtt{begin}\\[14.22636pt]
\vec{\mathsf{S}}(\mathtt{begin}(\mathsf{tr}(e)))&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.\\[22.76219pt]
\mathsf{snapshot}_{\textup{{RC}}}({h},\vec{\mathsf{S}},\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime},e,\xi)=&\mathsf{choice}\left(\left\{\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(c_{t^{\prime}})\left|\begin{array}[]{l}t^{\prime}\in h\land\\[2.84526pt]
c_{t^{\prime}}=\mathtt{commit}(t^{\prime})\land\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(c_{t^{\prime}})<\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(e)\land\\[2.84526pt]
\forall e^{\prime}.\left(\begin{array}[]{l}(e^{\prime},e)\in{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}\ \lor\\
(\mathsf{tr}(e^{\prime}),\mathsf{tr}(e))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\end{array}\right)\\[2.84526pt]
\hskip 42.67912pt\implies\vec{\mathsf{S}}(e^{\prime})\leq\vec{\mathsf{T}}(c_{t^{\prime}})\end{array}\right.\right\}\right)\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rl}\mathsf{readFrom}({h},\vec{\mathsf{T}},t,\delta)=&[x\mapsto(\textup{{{localWr}}}[x]\neq\bot)\,?\,\bot\ :\ w_{x}\text{ for each }x\in\mathsf{Keys}]\\[5.69054pt]
\text{ where }\textup{{{localWr}}}[x]=&\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}}\{e\ |\ \mathsf{tr}(e)=t\ \land\ {e}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\}\cup\{\bot\}\\[5.69054pt]
\text{ and }{w_{x}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\ \land&\vec{\mathsf{T}}(w_{x})=\max\left\{\vec{\mathsf{T}}(w^{\prime})\ \left|\ {\begin{array}[]{c}w^{\prime}\in\mathsf{events}({h})\ \land\ {w^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\ \land\\
\vec{\mathsf{T}}(\mathtt{commit}(\mathsf{tr}(w^{\prime})))\leq\delta\end{array}}\right.\right\}\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{validate}_{\textup{{SER}}}({h},\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime},t)=&\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\not\exists t^{\prime}\in h,x\in\mathsf{Keys}\text{ s.t. }({t}\ \mathsf{reads}\ {x}\lor{t}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x})\ \land\ {t^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\\[2.84526pt]
\hskip 18.49988pt\land\ \vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{begin}(t))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{commit}(t^{\prime}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{end}(t))\end{array}\right)\\[17.07164pt]
\mathsf{validate}_{\textup{{SI}}}({h},\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime},t)=&\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\not\exists t^{\prime}\in h,x\in\mathsf{Keys}\text{ s.t. }{t}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\ \land\ {t^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\ \land\\[2.84526pt]
\hskip 18.49988pt\land\ \vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{begin}(t))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{commit}(t^{\prime}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{end}(t))\end{array}\right)\\[17.07164pt]
\mathsf{validate}_{\textup{{RC}}}({h},\vec{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime},t)=&\mathsf{true}\\
\end{array}

Figure 0.A.3:
Definition of auxiliary functions for the operational semantics. The function
\mathsf{choice}
receives a set as input and returns one of its elements.

.

The proof of Theorem
3.1
is split in two parts: Lemma
2
and Lemma
4
. In Lemma
2
, we prove by induction that for any run
\rho
,
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
is a full history; using the auxiliary Lemma
1
about pending transactions. We then define in Equation
5
a relation on transactions that plays the role of consistency witness for
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
. Then, we prove in Lemma
3
that such relation is a commit order for
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
to conclude in Lemma
4
that
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
is indeed consistent. In all cases, we do a case-by-case analysis depending on which rule is employed during the inductive step.

For the sake of simplifying our notation, we denote by
\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,\rho^{\prime})
to the rule s.t. applied to run
\rho
on session
j
leads to configuration
\rho^{\prime}
.

Lemma 1

Let
\rho
be a run and
\mathsf{history}(\rho)={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
be its history. Any pending transaction in
T
is
({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})
-maximal.

Proof

We prove by induction on the length of a run
\rho
that any pending transaction is
({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})
-maximal; where
\mathsf{history}(\rho)={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
. The base case, where
\rho=\{c_{0}\}
and
c_{0}
is an initial configuration, is immediate by definition. Let us suppose that for every run of length at most
n
the property holds and let
\rho^{\prime}
a run of length
n+1
. As
\rho^{\prime}
is a sequence of configurations, there exist a reachable run
\rho
of length
n
, a session
j
and a rule
r
s.t.
r=\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,\rho^{\prime})
. Let us call
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
,
h^{\prime}={({T^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}})}
and
e
to
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
,
\mathsf{history}(\rho^{\prime})
and the last event in
{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}
-order belonging to
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
respectively. By induction hypothesis, any pending transaction in
h
is
({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})
-maximal. To conclude the inductive step, we show that for every possible rule
r
s.t.
r=\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,\rho^{\prime})
, the property also holds in
h^{\prime}
.

•

local
,
if-false
,
if-true
,
insert
,
commit
,
abort
: The result trivially holds as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
and
\mathsf{complete}({T^{\prime}})\subseteq\mathsf{complete}({T})
.

•

begin
: We observe that in this case,
T=T\cup\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}
,
\mathsf{reads}({T^{\prime}})=\mathsf{reads}({T})
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup\{{({t^{\prime},\textup{{last}}(h,j)})}\ |\ \mathtt{ses}({t^{\prime}})=j\}
. Thus,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is pending and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal. Moreover, as described in Figure
0.A.1
,
\vec{\mathsf{B}}(j)=\epsilon
; so there is no other transaction in session
j
that is pending. Hence, as
T^{\prime}\setminus\mathsf{complete}({T^{\prime}})=T\mathsf{complete}({T})\cup\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}
, by induction hypothesis, every pending transaction is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal.

•

select
,
update
,
delete
:
Figure

0.A.2
describes
h^{\prime}
by the equation
h^{\prime}=(h\oplus_{j}{({e,\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,s)})})\bigoplus_{x\in\mathsf{Keys},\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]\neq\bot}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x],e)
; where
e
is the new event executed and
\vec{\mathsf{w}}
is defined following the descriptions in Figures
0.A.2
and
0.A.3
.
In this case,
T^{\prime}=T,\mathsf{reads}({T^{\prime}})=\mathsf{reads}({T})\cup\{e\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
,
\forall x\in\mathsf{Keys}
s.t.
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]=\bot
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
and
\forall x\in\mathsf{Keys}
s.t.
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]\neq\bot
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}\cup\{(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x],e)\}
. Note that as described by Figure
0.A.3
, in the latter case, when
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]\neq\bot
,
\mathsf{tr}(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x])\in\mathsf{complete}({T})=\mathsf{complete}({T^{\prime}})
. In conclusion, using the induction hypothesis, we also conclude that every pending transaction is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal.

∎

Lemma 2

For every run
\rho
,
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
is a full history.

Proof

We prove by induction on the length of a run
\rho
that
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
is a full history; where the base case,
\rho=\{c_{0}\}
and
c_{0}
is an initial configuration, is trivial by definition. Let us suppose that for every run of length at most
n
the property holds and let
\rho^{\prime}
a run of length
n+1
. As
\rho^{\prime}
is a sequence of configurations, there exist a reachable run
\rho
of length
n
, a session
j
and a rule
r
s.t.
r=\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,\rho^{\prime})
. Let us call
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
,
h^{\prime}={({T^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}})}
and
e
to
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
,
\mathsf{history}(\rho^{\prime})
and the last event in
{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}
-order belonging to
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
respectively. By induction hypothesis,
h
is a full history. To conclude the inductive step, we show that for every possible rule
r
s.t.
r=\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,\rho^{\prime})
, the history
h^{\prime}
is also a full history. In particular, by definitions
1
and
2
, it suffices to prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
is an acyclic relation and that for every variable
x
and read event
r
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}}^{-1}(r)\downarrow
if and only if
r
does not read
x
from a local write and in such case,
\mathtt{value}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}}^{-1}(r),x)\neq\bot
.

•

local
,
if-false
,
if-true
,
insert
,
commit
,
abort
: The result trivially holds as
\mathsf{reads}({T^{\prime}})=\mathsf{reads}({T}),{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
; using that
h
is consistent.

•

begin
: We observe that
h^{\prime}=h\oplus_{j}{({e,\mathtt{begin}})}
, so
T=T\cup\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}
,
\mathsf{reads}({T^{\prime}})=\mathsf{reads}({T})
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup\{{({t^{\prime},\textup{{last}}(h,j)})}\ |\ \mathtt{ses}({t^{\prime}})=j\}
. In such case, by Lemma
1
,
t
is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal. Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
is acyclic as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
is also acyclic. Finally, as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, we conclude
that
h^{\prime}
is a full history.

•

select
,
update
,
delete
: Here
h^{\prime}=(h\oplus_{j}{({e,\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,s)})})\bigoplus_{x\in\mathsf{Keys},\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]\neq\bot}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x],e)
where
e
is the new event executed and
\vec{\mathsf{w}}
is defined following the descriptions in Figures
0.A.2
and
0.A.3
. In this case,
T^{\prime}=T,\mathsf{reads}({T^{\prime}})=\mathsf{reads}({T})\cup\{e\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
,
\forall x\in\mathsf{Keys}
s.t.
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]=\bot
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
and
\forall x\in\mathsf{Keys}
s.t.
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]\neq\bot
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}\cup\{(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x],e)\}
. Note that as the timestamp of any event is always positive and
\vec{\mathsf{T}}(\textup{{{init}}})=0
; for any key
x
,
\mathsf{w}[x]\neq\bot
if and only if
\textup{{{localWr}}}[x]=\bot
. Thus,
\vec{\mathsf{w}}
is well defined, and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}}^{-1}(r)\downarrow
if and only
\textup{{{localWr}}}[x]=\bot
. In such case, as any event
w
writes on a key
x
if and only if
\mathtt{value}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}}(w,x)\neq\bot
, we conclude that
\mathtt{value}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}}^{-1}(r),x)\neq\bot
. To conclude the result, we need to show that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
is acyclic.
As
\rho
is reachable, by Figure
0.A.3
’s definition we know that for any event
r
and key
x
, if
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)\downarrow
,
\mathsf{tr}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r))\in\mathsf{cmtt}({h})
. Thus, by Lemma
1
,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal as it is not committed. Therefore, by the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
, as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
is acyclic and
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
is also acyclic. In conclusion,
h^{\prime}
is a full history.

∎

Once proven that for any run
\rho
,
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
is a full history, we need to prove that there exists a commit order
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
that witnesses
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
consistency. Equation
5
defines a relation that we prove in Lemma
3
that it is a total order for
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
.

(t,t^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}t\in\mathsf{complete}({T})\land t^{\prime}\in\mathsf{complete}({T})\ \land\\
\qquad\vec{\mathsf{T}}(\mathtt{end}(t))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}(\mathtt{end}(t^{\prime}))\\
t\in\mathsf{complete}({T})\land t^{\prime}\not\in\mathsf{complete}({T})\\
t\not\in\mathsf{complete}({T})\land t^{\prime}\not\in\mathsf{complete}({T})\ \land\\
\qquad\vec{\mathsf{T}}(\mathtt{begin}(t))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}(\mathtt{begin}(t^{\prime}))\\
\end{array}\right.

(5)

Lemma 3

For every run
\rho
, the relation
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
defined above is a commit order for
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
.

Proof

We prove by induction on the length of a run
\rho
that the relation
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
defined by the equation below is a commit order for
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
, i.e., if
\mathsf{history}(\rho)={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
.

The base case, where
\rho
is composed only by an initial configuration is immediate as in such case
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}=\emptyset
. Let us suppose that for every run of length at most
n
the property holds and let
\rho^{\prime}
a run of length
n+1
. As
\rho^{\prime}
is a sequence of configurations, there exist a reachable run
\rho
of length
n
, a session
j
and a rule
\mathsf{r}
s.t.
\mathsf{r}=\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,\rho^{\prime})
. Let us call
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
,
h^{\prime}={({T^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}})}
and
e
to
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
,
\mathsf{history}(\rho^{\prime})
and the last event in
{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}
-order belonging to
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
respectively. By induction hypothesis,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
is a commit order for
h
. To conclude the inductive step, we show that
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
is also a commit order for
h^{\prime}
.

•

local
,
if-false
,
if-true
: As
h=h^{\prime}
and
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}=\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
. Thus, the result trivially holds.

•

begin
: In this case,
e=\mathtt{begin}(\textup{{last}}(h,j))
and
\textup{{last}}(h,j)\not\in\mathsf{complete}({T_{\rho^{\prime}}})
. Note that for any event
e^{\prime}\neq e
,
\vec{\mathsf{T}}(e)>\vec{\mathsf{T}}(e^{\prime})
and
\mathsf{complete}({T_{\rho}^{\prime}})=\mathsf{complete}({\rho})
. Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}\cup\{(t^{\prime},\textup{{last}}(h^{\prime},j))\ |\ t^{\prime}\in T\}
. As
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup\{(t^{\prime},\textup{{last}}(h,j))\ |\ \mathtt{ses}({t^{\prime}})=j\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
; so
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
is a commit order for
h^{\prime}
.

•

insert
: In this case, as
\mathsf{complete}({T_{\rho}^{\prime}})=\mathsf{complete}({T_{\rho}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
. Hence, as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
.

•

select
,
update
,
delete
: Once again, as
\mathsf{complete}({T_{\rho}^{\prime}})=\mathsf{complete}({T_{\rho}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
. Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
,
\forall x\in\mathsf{Keys}
s.t.
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]=\bot
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
and
\forall x\in\mathsf{Keys}
s.t.
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]\neq\bot
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}\cup\{(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x],e)\}
. In the latter case, where
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]\neq\bot
, we know that
\mathsf{tr}(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x])\in\mathsf{complete}({T})
thanks to the definitions on Figure
0.A.3
. By Equation
5
, as
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is pending, we deduce that
(\mathsf{tr}(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]),\mathsf{tr}(e))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
. Therefore, as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
, we conclude that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
.

•

commit
,
abort
: In this case,
e=\mathtt{end}{\textup{{last}}(h,j)}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}\restriction_{T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}\restriction_{T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
. Thus, to prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
we only need to discuss about
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
. By Lemma
1
,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal. Hence, we focus on proving that for any transaction
t^{\prime}
s.t.
(t^{\prime},\textup{{last}}(h,j))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
,
(t^{\prime},\textup{{last}}(h,j))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
. Any such transaction
t^{\prime}
must be completed by Lemma
1
. However, by the definition on Figure
0.A.1
, we know that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}(e)>\vec{\mathsf{T}}(\mathtt{end}(t^{\prime}))
, so
(t^{\prime},\textup{{last}}(h,j))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
by Equation
5
. Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
.

∎

Lemma 4

For every total run
\rho
, the
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
is consistent.

Proof

Let
\rho^{T}
be a total run. By Lemma
2
,
\mathsf{history}(\rho^{T})
is a full history. Thus, to prove that
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
is consistent, by Definition
6
, we need to show that there exists a commit order
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
that witnesses its consistency. We prove by induction on the length of a prefix
\rho
of a total run
\rho^{T}
that the relation
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
defined in Equation
5
is a commit order that witnesses
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
’s consistency. Note that by Lemma
3
, the relation
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
is indeed a commit order.

The base case, where
\rho
is composed only by an initial configuration is immediate as in such case
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}=\emptyset
. Let us suppose that for every run of length at most
n
the property holds and let
\rho^{\prime}
a run of length
n+1
. As
\rho^{\prime}
is a sequence of configurations, there exist a reachable run
\rho
of length
n
, a session
j
and a rule
\mathsf{r}
s.t.
\mathsf{r}=\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,\rho^{\prime})
. Let us call
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
,
h^{\prime}={({T^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}})}
and
e
to
\mathsf{history}(\rho)
,
\mathsf{history}(\rho^{\prime})
and the last event in
{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}
-order belonging to
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
respectively. By induction hypothesis,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
is a commit order that witnesses
h
’s consistency. To conclude the inductive step, we show that for every possible rule
\mathsf{r}
s.t.
\mathsf{r}=\mathsf{rule}(\rho,j,\rho^{\prime})
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
is a commit order witnessing
h^{\prime}
’s consistency.

By contradiction, let suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
does not witness
h^{\prime}
’s consistency. Then, there exists a variable
x
, a read event
r
, an axiom
a\in\iota
and two committed transactions
t_{1},t_{2}
s.t.
(t_{1},e)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
,
{t_{2}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}
,
\mathsf{vis}_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}(t_{2},r,x)
holds in
h^{\prime}
but
(t_{1},t_{2})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
; where
\iota=\vec{\mathsf{I}}(\mathtt{begin}(e))
. Thus, if we prove that such dependencies can be seen in
h
using
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
, we obtain a contradiction as
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
witnesses
h
’s consistency. Note that as shown during the proof of Lemma
3
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}\restriction_{T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}\restriction_{T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}}
; so we simply prove that
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
cannot be
t_{1}
,
t_{2}
,
\mathsf{tr}(r)
or any intermediate transaction causing
\mathsf{vis}_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}(t_{2},r,x)
to hold in
h^{\prime}
.

•

local
,
if-false
,
if-true
: As
h=h^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
, this case is impossible.

•

begin
: In this case,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}\cup\{(t^{\prime},\textup{{last}}(h^{\prime},j))\ |\ t^{\prime}\in T\}
. By Lemma
3
,
\textup{{last}}(h^{\prime},j)
is
({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime})
-maximal, so
\textup{{last}}(h^{\prime},j)\neq t_{1}
. Moreover,
\mathsf{reads}({\textup{{last}}(h^{\prime},j)})=\emptyset
, so
r\neq\mathsf{reads}({\textup{{last}}(h^{\prime},j)})
. In addition,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)\neq t_{2}
as
\mathsf{writes}({\textup{{last}}(h,j)})=\emptyset
.

–

a=\textsf{Serializability},\textsf{Prefix}
or
Read Committed
: In all cases, the axioms do not relate any other transactions besides
t_{1},t_{2}
and
\mathsf{tr}(r)
, so this case is impossible.

–

a=\textsf{Conflict}
: In this case,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)\neq t_{4}
as it is
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
-maximal; so this case is also impossible.

•

insert
: In this case,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
. Moreover,
\mathsf{reads}({T^{\prime}})=\mathsf{reads}({T})
,
\mathsf{writes}({T^{\prime}})=\mathsf{writes}({T})
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
. Thus, this case is also impossible.

•

select
,
update
,
delete
: In this case,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
,
\forall x\in\mathsf{Keys}
s.t.
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]=\bot
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
and
\forall x\in\mathsf{Keys}
s.t.
\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x]\neq\bot
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}_{x}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}\cup\{(\vec{\mathsf{w}}[x],e)\}
.
As
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is pending, by Lemma
3
,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)\neq t_{1}
as it is
({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime})
-maximal. Moreover, as
\mathsf{writes}({\textup{{last}}(h,j)})=\emptyset
,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)\neq t_{2}
. Then, we analyze if
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
can be
\mathsf{tr}(r)
(and thus,
r=e
) or any intermediate transaction. Note that for all three isolation levels we study,
\mathsf{readFrom}
returns the value written by the transaction with the last commit timestamp for a given snapshot time. Hence, as
(t_{1},r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
and
(t_{2},\mathsf{tr}(r))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
, we deduce that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))>\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho}(\mathtt{begin}(\textup{{last}}(h,j)))
. We continue the analysis distinguishing between one case per axiom:

–

a=\textsf{Serializability}
: As
\rho^{\prime}
is a prefix of a total run
\rho^{T}
, there exists runs
\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathsf{rule}(\hat{\rho},j^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime})
is either
commit
or
abort
and both a prefix of
\rho^{T}
; where
j^{\prime}
is the session of
\mathsf{tr}(r)
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\hat{\rho}
and
\hat{\rho}^{\prime}
have minimal size; so
\textup{{last}}(\mathsf{history}(\hat{\rho}),j^{\prime})=\mathsf{tr}(r)
. As
\rho^{T}
is total and
\hat{\rho}^{\prime}
is a prefix of
\rho^{T}
,
\mathsf{validate}_{\iota}(\mathsf{history}(\hat{\rho},\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}},\mathsf{tr}(r)))
holds.

By the monotonicity of
\vec{\mathsf{T}}
,
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}\subseteq\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}
. Hence, as
(t_{1},r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
and

\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{1}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))
,
by the definitions of Figure
0.A.2
and Figure
0.A.3
we deduce that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{begin}(\mathsf{tr}(r)))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))
.
However, as
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{begin}(\mathsf{tr}(r)))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{end}(\mathsf{tr}(r)))
,
{\mathsf{tr}(r)}\ \mathsf{reads}\ {x}
,
{t_{2}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}
; we conclude that
\mathsf{validate}_{\textup{{SER}}}(\mathsf{history}(\hat{\rho}^{\prime}),\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}},\mathsf{tr}(r))
does not hold; so this case is impossible.

–

a=\textsf{Conflict}
: In this case,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
cannot be an intermediate transaction nor
\mathsf{tr}(r)
as
\mathsf{writes}({\textup{{last}}(h,j)})=\emptyset
; so this case is also impossible.

–

a=\textsf{Prefix}
: In this case,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
cannot be an intermediate transaction as by Lemma
1
,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal. Thus,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
must be
\mathsf{tr}(r)
and
e=r
. Therefore, there exists a transaction
t_{4}
s.t.
(t_{2},t_{4})\in{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}^{*}
and
(t_{4},\textup{{last}}(h,j))\in({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime})
. Note that
t_{4}
must be committed and that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{4}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{begin}(\textup{{last}}(h,j)))
. Hence, as
(t_{2},t_{4})\in{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}^{*}
and
(t_{1},t_{2})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
and they are both committed, we deduce that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{4}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{begin}(\textup{{last}}(h,j)))
. However, this contradicts that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))>\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{begin}(\textup{{last}}(h,j)))
Thus, this case is impossible.

–

a=\textsf{Read Committed}
: In this case,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
must be
\mathsf{tr}(r)
and in particular,
e=r
. As depicted on Figure
0.A.2
and Figure
0.A.3
, as
(t_{1},r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
,
\vec{\mathsf{S}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(e)\leq\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(t_{1})
. However, as
(t_{1},t_{2})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
,
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{1}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))
. Hence, as
(t_{2},e)\in({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}});{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}^{*}
, there exists an event
e^{\prime}\in\textup{{last}}(h,j)
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}^{*}
and
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(e^{\prime})
. However, by
\mathsf{snapshot}_{\textup{{RC}}}
’s definition,
\vec{\mathsf{S}}(e^{\prime})\leq\vec{\mathsf{S}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(e)
; so we deduce that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{1}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))<\vec{\mathsf{S}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(e)
. This contradicts the definition of
\mathsf{readFrom}
; so this case is impossible.

•

commit
,
abort
: In this case,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}\restriction(T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\})={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}\restriction(T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\})
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
. First, using that by induction hypothesis any prefix
\tilde{\rho}
of
\rho
is consistent using
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\tilde{\rho}}
; we define
\tilde{\rho}
the prefix of
\rho
that introduces the read event
r
. As
\mathsf{history}(\tilde{\rho})={({\tilde{T},\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}},\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}}})}
is consistent and
(t_{1},r)\in\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}}_{x}
;
t_{1}
is committed. Hence, by the definitions of
\mathsf{readFrom}
and
\mathsf{snapshot}_{\iota}
on Figure
0.A.3
and the rules semantics on Figure
0.A.2
, we deduce that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{1}))>\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho}(\mathtt{begin}(t_{2}))
. Next, as
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is pending in
h
, it is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
-maximal. Therefore, it is also
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
-maximal; so it cannot play the role of
t_{1}
. However, it can play the role of
t_{2}
,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
or the role of an intermediate transaction. Let us analyze case by case depending on the axiom:

–

a=\textsf{Serializability}
: Two sub-cases arise:

*

\textup{{last}}(h,j)=t_{2}
: I this case,
{t_{2}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}
must hold. As
\rho^{\prime}
is a prefix of a total run
\rho^{T}
, there exists runs
\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathsf{rule}(\hat{\rho},j^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime})
is either
commit
or
abort
and both a prefix of
\rho^{T}
; where
j^{\prime}
is the session of
\mathsf{tr}(r)
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\hat{\rho}
and
\hat{\rho}^{\prime}
have minimal size; so
\textup{{last}}(\mathsf{history}(\hat{\rho}),j^{\prime})=\mathsf{tr}(r)
. As
\rho^{T}
is total and
\hat{\rho}^{\prime}
is a prefix of
\rho^{T}
,
\mathsf{validate}_{\iota}(\mathsf{history}(\hat{\rho},\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}},\mathsf{tr}(r)))
holds. Note that as
(t_{1},t_{2})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
and they are both committed,
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{1}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))
. However,
{\mathsf{tr}(r)}\ \mathsf{reads}\ {x}
,
{t_{2}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}
and
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{begin}(\mathsf{tr}(r)))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(\textup{{last}}(h,j)))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(\mathsf{tr}(r)))
; which contradicts that
\mathsf{validate}_{\iota}(\mathsf{history}(\hat{\rho},\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime}},\mathsf{tr}(r)))
holds. In conclusion, this case is impossible.

*

\textup{{last}}(h,j)=\mathsf{tr}(r)
: In such case, as
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
are committed,
(t_{2},\textup{{last}}(h,j))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
and
(t_{1},t_{2})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
. Hence, this case is also impossible as
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
witnesses that
h
is consistent.

–

a=\textsf{Prefix}
: In this case, there exists a transaction
t_{4}
s.t.
(t_{2},t_{4})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{*}
and
(t_{4},\mathsf{tr}(r))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
. As
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is pending in
h
, by Lemma
1
,
({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})
-maximal. Thus, as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
,
t_{4}\neq\textup{{last}}(h,j)
. Moreover, as
(t_{2},t_{4})\in{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}^{*}
,
t_{4}
is committed and
\textup{{last}}(h,j)\neq t_{4}
is the
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
-maximal transaction that is committed,
t_{2}\neq\textup{{last}}(h,j)
. Hence,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)=\mathsf{tr}(r)
. However, as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}\restriction_{T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}\restriction_{T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}}
; we conclude that
(t_{1},t_{2})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
,
(t_{2},t_{4})\in{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}}^{*}
and
(t_{4},\textup{{last}}(h,j))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
; which contradicts that
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
witnesses
h
’s consistency, so this case is impossible.

–

a=\textsf{Conflict}
: In this case, there exists a variable
y
and a transaction
t_{4}
s.t.
{t_{4}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {y}
,
{\mathsf{tr}(r)}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {y}

(t_{2},t_{4})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{*}
,
(t_{4},\mathsf{tr}(r))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
. As
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is the
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
-maximal transaction that is committed,
(t_{2},\mathsf{tr}(r)),(t_{4},\mathsf{tr}(r))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
and
\mathsf{writes}({\mathsf{tr}(r)})\neq\emptyset
, we deduce that
\textup{{last}}(h,j)\neq t_{2},t_{4}
. Hence,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
must be
\mathsf{tr}(r)
and
e=\mathtt{commit}(\textup{{last}}(h,j))
. On one hand, we observe that as
(t_{4},\textup{{last}}(h,j))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
and they are both committed,
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{4}))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(e)
. On the other hand, as
(t_{2},t_{4})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{*}
and
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{begin}(\mathsf{tr}(r)))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{2}))
; we conclude that
\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{begin}(\mathsf{tr}(r)))<\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathtt{commit}(t_{4}))
. In conclusion, we obtain that
\mathsf{validate}_{\textup{{SI}}}(h^{\prime},\vec{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho^{\prime}},\textup{{last}}(h,j))
does not hold due to the existence of
t_{4}
; which contradict the hypothesis, so this case is impossible.

–

a=\textsf{Read Committed}
: In this case,
r\neq e
as
r
is a read event and
e
is not, and
(t_{2},r)\in({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime});{{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}^{\prime}}^{*}
. Hence, as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}\cup\{(e^{\prime},e)\ |\ e^{\prime}\in\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}
;
(t_{2},r)\in({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}});{\color[rgb]{0.7695,0.1172,0.2266}\mathsf{po}}^{*}
. Finally, as
\textup{{last}}(h,j)
is pending in
h
,
\textup{{last}}(h,j)\neq t_{2}
. Thus, as
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}\restriction_{T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}\restriction_{T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}\times T\setminus\{\textup{{last}}(h,j)\}}
; we deduce that
(t_{1},t_{2})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
. However, this contradicts that
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho}
witnesses
h
’s consistency; so this case is also impossible.

As every possible case is impossible, we deduce that the hypothesis,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
does not witnesses
h^{\prime}
’s consistency is false; so we conclude the proof of the inductive step.

∎

Appendix 0.B
Proofs of
Theorems

4.1
,
4.3
,
4.2
and
5.1

0.B.1
Complexity analysis of
Algorithms

1
and
2
(Proof of
Theorem

4.1
)

For a given history
h
,
Algorithm

1
computes necessary and sufficient conditions for an execution of
h

\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}})}
to be consistent. It computes a bigger relation
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}
that includes
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
and any other dependency between transactions that can be deduced from the isolation configuration.
Algorithm

1
decides if
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
is a commit order witnessing consistency of the history (
Lemma

5
) and it runs in polynomial time (
Lemma

7
).

Lemma 5

For any full history
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
, the execution
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}})}
is consistent if and only if
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}=\textup{{saturate}}(h,{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}})
is acyclic.

Proof

Let
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
be a history,
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}})}
be an execution of
h
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}=\textup{{saturate}}(h,{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}})
be the relation obtained thanks to
Algorithm

1
.

\implies
Let us suppose that
\xi
is consistent. As
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
is acyclic, it suffice to prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
. By contradiction, let us suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
. As
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}
(line
3
), there exists
t_{1},t_{2}
s.t.
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
. In such case, such tuple must be added in line
8
. Hence, there exists
x\in\mathsf{Keys},e\in\mathsf{reads}({h})
and
v\in\mathsf{vis}(\mathsf{iso}(h)(\mathsf{tr}(r)))
s.t.
t_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)
and
\mathsf{vis}_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}}(t_{2},r,x)
holds in
h
. As
\xi
is consistent,
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
; which is impossible. Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
.

\impliedby

Let us suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}
is acyclic. By contradiction, let us suppose that
\xi
is not consistent. Then, there exists an read event
r
s.t.
C_{\mathsf{iso}(h)(\mathsf{tr}(r))}^{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}}(r)
does not hold. Hence, by
Equation

1
, there exists
v\in\mathsf{vis}(\mathsf{iso}(h)(\mathsf{tr}(r)))
,
x\in\mathsf{Keys}
,
t_{2}\in T
s.t.
\mathsf{v}({\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}})(t_{2},r,x)
hold in
h
but
(t_{2},t_{1})\not\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
; where
t_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)
. In such case,
Algorithm

1
ensures in line
8
that
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}
. However, as
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}
(line
3
),
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
is a total order and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}
is acyclic,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}_{\textup{{{res}}}}
. Thus,
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
; which is impossible. Thus,
\xi
is consistent.

∎

Lemma 6

Let
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
be a history s.t.
\mathsf{iso}(h)
is bounded by
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
x\in\mathsf{Keys}
be a key,
t\in T
be a transaction,
r
be a read event,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}\subseteq T\times T
be a partial order and
v
be a visibility relation in
\mathsf{vis}(\mathsf{iso}(h)(\mathsf{tr}(r)))
. Evaluating
\mathsf{v}({\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}})(t,r,x)
is in
\mathcal{O}(|h|^{k-2})
.

Proof

As
\mathsf{iso}(h)
is bounded, there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
s.t.
|\mathsf{vis}(\mathsf{iso}(h)(t))|\leq k
. Hence, the number of quantifiers employed by a visibility relation is at most
k
(and at least
3
according to Equation
1
). In addition, for each
\mathsf{v}\in\mathsf{vis}(\mathsf{iso}(h)(t))
evaluating each condition
\mathsf{v}({\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}})(t,r,x)
can be modelled with an algorithm that employ
k-3
nested loops, one per existential quantifier employed by
\mathsf{v}
, and that for each quantifier assignment evaluates the quantifier-free part of the formula.

First, we observe that as
\mathsf{WrCons}
predicate only query information about the
k-1
quantified events, the size of such sub-formula is in
\mathcal{O}(k)
. Next, we notice that as
\mathtt{WHERE}
predicate can be evaluated in constant time, for every key
x
and event
w
, computing
\mathtt{value}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}}(x,w)
is in
\mathcal{O}(k\cdot T)
. Hence, as
k
is constant, evaluating the quantifier-free formula of
v
is in
\mathcal{O}(|h|)
and thus, evaluating
\mathsf{v}({\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}})(t,r,x)
is in
\mathcal{O}(|h|^{k-3}\cdot|h|)=\mathcal{O}(|h|^{k-2})
.

∎

Lemma 7

Let
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
be a full history,
k
be a bound on
\mathsf{iso}(h)
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}\subseteq T\times T
be a partial order. Algorithm
1
runs in
\mathcal{O}(|h|^{k+1})
.

Proof

Let
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
be a full history.
Algorithm

1
can be decomposed in two blocks: lines
4
-
8
and lines
6
-
8
. Hence, the cost of Algorithm
1
is in
\mathcal{O}(|\mathsf{Keys}|\cdot|\mathsf{events}({h})|\cdot|T|\cdot U)
; where
U
is an upper-bound of the cost of evaluating lines
6
-
8
. On one hand, both
|\mathsf{Keys}|
,
|\mathsf{events}({h})|
and
|T|
are in
\mathcal{O}(|h|)
. On the other hand, as
\mathsf{iso}(h)
is bounded by
k
, by
Lemma

6
,
U\in\mathcal{O}(|h|^{k-2})
. Altogether, we deduce that
Algorithm

1
runs in
\mathcal{O}(|h|^{k+1})
.

∎

Algorithm
2
generalizes the results for
RA
and
RC
in
[
6
]
for full histories with heterogeneous saturable isolation configurations; proving that such histories can be checked in polynomial time.
See
4.1

We split the proof of Theorem
4.1
in two Lemmas: Lemma
8
that proves the correctness of Algorithm
2
and Lemma
9
that ensures its polynomial-time behavior.

Lemma 8

For every full history
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
whose isolation configuration is saturable, Algorithm
2
returns
\mathsf{true}
if and only if
h
is consistent.

Proof

Let
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
a full history whose isolation configuration is saturable and let
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}
be the visibility relation defined in line
3
in
Algorithm

2
.

On one hand, let suppose that
h
is consistent and let
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}})}
be a consistent execution of
h
. If we show that
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
, we can conclude that Algorithm
2
returns
\mathsf{true}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
is acyclic.
Let
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}
and let us prove that
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
. As
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
, by the definition of commit order, we can assume that
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}\setminus({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})
. In such case, there must exists
x\in\mathsf{Keys},e\in\mathsf{reads}({h})
and
\mathsf{v}\in\mathsf{vis}(\mathsf{iso}(h)(\mathsf{tr}(e)))
s.t.
{t_{2}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}
and
\mathsf{v}(({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})^{+})(t_{2},e,x)
holds.
As
\mathsf{iso}(h)(\mathsf{tr}(e))
is saturable,
\mathsf{v}(({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})^{+})(t_{2},e,x)
holds. Hence, as
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
is a commit order and
({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})^{+}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
;
\mathsf{v}({\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}})(t_{2},e,x)
also holds. Therefore, as
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
witnesses
h
’s consistency, we deduce that
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
.

On the other hand, let us suppose that Algorithm
2
returns
\mathsf{true}
. Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}
must be acyclic by the condition in line
4
. Therefore, as
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}
is acyclic it can be extended to a total order
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
. Let us prove that the execution
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}})}
is consistent. Let
x\in\mathsf{Keys},t_{2}\in T,e\in\mathsf{reads}({h})
and
\mathsf{v}\in\mathsf{vis}(\mathsf{iso}(h)(\mathsf{tr}(e)))
s.t.
{t_{2}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}
and
\mathsf{v}({\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}})(t_{2},e,x)
holds. As Algorithm
2
returns
\mathsf{true}
, we deduce that Algorithm
1
checks the condition at line
7
. As
\mathsf{iso}(h)(\mathsf{tr}(e))
is saturable,
\mathsf{v}(({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}})^{+})(t_{2},e,x)
also holds. Thus,
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}
. As
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
,
(t_{2},t_{1})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
; so
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}
witnesses
h
’s consistency.

∎

Lemma 9

For every full history
h
whose isolation configuration is bounded, Algorithm
2
runs in polynomial time with respect
\mathcal{O}(|h|)
.

Proof

Let
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
be a full history whose isolation configuration is saturable.
First, we observe that checking if a graph
G=(V,E)
is acyclic can be easily done with a DFS in
\mathcal{O}(|V|+|E|)
. Thus, the cost of checking acyclicity of both
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
(line
2
) and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{pco}}
(line
4
) is in
\mathcal{O}(|T|+|T|^{2})=\mathcal{O}(|T|^{2})\subseteq\mathcal{O}(|h|^{2})
. Furthermore, by Lemma
7
, the cost of executing Algorithm
1
is in
\mathcal{O}(|h|^{k+1})
; where
k
is a bound in
\mathsf{iso}(h)
. Thus, checking
h
’s consistency with Algorithm
2
can be done in polynomial time.

∎

0.B.2
Proof of
Theorem

4.2

See
4.2

The proof of
Theorem

4.2
is structured in two parts: proving that the problem is in NP and proving that is NP-hard. The first part corresponds to
Lemma

10
; which is analogous as the proof of Lemma
18
. The second part, based on a reduction to
1
-in-
3
SAT problem, corresponds to
Lemmas

11
,
13
and
17
.

Lemma 10

The problem of checking consistency for a bounded width client history
h
with an isolation configuration stronger than
RC
and
\mathtt{width}(h)\geq 3
is in NP.

Proof

Let
h={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
a client history whose isolation configuration is stronger than
RC
. Guessing a witness of
h
,
\overline{h}
, and an execution of
\overline{h}
,
\xi={({\overline{h},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}}})}
, can be done in
\mathcal{O}(|\mathsf{Keys}|\cdot|\mathsf{events}({h})|^{2}+|T|^{2})\subseteq\mathcal{O}(|h|^{3})
. By
Lemma

5
, checking if
\xi
is consistent is equivalent as checking if
\textup{{saturate}}(h^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{co}})
is an acyclic relation. As by
Lemma

7
,
Algorithm

1
requires polynomial time, we conclude the result.

∎

For showing NP-hardness, we will reduce
1
-in-
3
SAT to checking consistency. Let
\varphi
be a boolean formula with
n
clauses and
m
variables of the form
\varphi=\bigwedge\limits_{i=1}^{n}(v_{i}^{0}\lor v_{i}^{1}\lor v_{i}^{2})
; we construct a history
h_{\varphi}
s.t.
h_{\varphi}
is consistent if and only if
\varphi
is satisfiable with exactly only one variable assigned the value
\mathsf{true}
. The key idea is designing a history with width
3
that is stratified in
rounds
, one per clause. In each round, three transactions, one per variable in the clause, “compete” to be first in the commit order. The one that precedes the other two correspond to the variable in
\varphi
that is satisfied.

First, we define the round
0
corresponding to the variables of
\varphi
. For every variable
x_{i}\in\mathtt{var}(\varphi),1\leq i\leq m
we define an homonymous key
x_{i}
that represents such variable. Doing an abuse of notation, we say that
x_{i}\in\mathtt{var}(\varphi)
. Then, we create two transactions
1_{i}
and
0_{i}
associated to the two states of
x_{i}
,
1
and
0
. The former contains the event
\mathtt{INSERT}({\{x_{i}:1,1_{i}:1\}})
while the latter
\mathtt{INSERT}({\{x_{i}:0,0_{i}:1\}})
. Both
1_{i}
and
0_{i}
write also on a special key named
1_{i}
and
0_{i}
respectively to indicate on the database that they have committed.

Next, we define rounds
1-n
representing each clause in
\varphi
. For each clause
C_{i}\coloneqq(v_{i}^{0}\lor v_{i}^{1}\lor v_{i}^{2}),1\leq i\leq n
, we define the round
i
. Round
i
is composed by three transactions:
t_{i}^{0}
,
t_{i}^{1}
and
t_{i}^{2}
, representing the choice of the variable among
v_{i}^{0},v_{i}^{1}
and
v_{i}^{2}
that is selected in the clause
C_{i}
. Transactions
t_{i}^{j}
write on keys
v_{i}^{j}
and
v_{i}^{j+1\bmod 3}
to preserve the structure of the clause
C_{i}
, as well on the special homonymous key
t_{i}^{j}
to indicate that such transaction has been executed; in a similar way as we did in the round
0
. For that, we impose that transactions
t_{i}^{j}
are composed of an event
\mathtt{SELECT}({\lambda x:\mathtt{eq}(x,v_{i}^{j},v_{i}^{j+1\bmod 3},v_{i}^{j+2\bmod 3})})
followed by an event
\mathtt{INSERT}({\{v_{i}^{j}:0,v_{i}^{j+1\bmod 3}:1,t_{i}^{j}:-1\}})
.

The function
\mathtt{eq}:\mathsf{Rows}\times\mathsf{Keys}^{3}\to\{\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false}\}
is described in Equation
6
and assumes that
\mathsf{Rows}
contains two distinct values
0
and
1
and that there is a predicate
\mathtt{val}:\mathsf{Rows}\to\{0,1\}
that returns the value of a variable in the database. Intuitively, for any key
r
, if
a,b,c
correspond to the three variables in a clause
C_{i}
(possibly permuted), whenever
\lnot\mathtt{eq}(r,a,b,c)
holds, we deduce that the value assigned at key
a
is
1
while on the other two keys the assigned value is
0
. Moreover, whenever
r
refers to any of the special keys such as
0_{i},1_{i}
or
t_{i}^{j}
, the predicate
\mathtt{eq}(r,a,b,c)
always holds.

\mathtt{eq}(r,a,b,c)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathtt{val}(r)\neq 1&\text{if }\mathsf{key}(r)=a\\
\mathtt{val}(r)\neq 0&\text{if }\mathsf{key}(r)=b\lor\mathsf{key}(r)=c\\
\mathsf{true}&\text{if }\mathsf{key}(r)\in\{t_{i}^{j}\ |\ 1\leq i\leq n,0\leq j\leq 2\}\\
\mathsf{true}&\text{if }\mathsf{key}(r)\in\{1_{i},0_{i}\ |\ 1\leq i\leq m\}\\
\mathsf{false}&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

(6)

Finally, we add an initial transaction that writes on every key the value
1
. For that, we assume that
\mathsf{Keys}
contains only one key per variable used in
\varphi
as well as one key per aforementioned transaction. We denote by
T
the set of all described transactions as well as by
\mathtt{round}(t)
to the round a transaction
t\in T
belongs to.

We describe the session order in the history
h_{\varphi}
using an auxiliary relation
\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}}
. We establish that
(1_{i},1_{j}),(0_{i},0_{j})\in\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}}
for any pair of indices
i,j,1\leq i<j\leq m
. We also enforce that
(t_{i}^{j},t_{i+1}^{j})\in\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}}
, for every
1\leq i\leq n,0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq 2
. Finally, we connect round
0
with round
1
by enforcing that
(1_{m},t_{1}^{0})\in\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}}
and
(0_{m},t_{1}^{1})\in\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}}
. Then, we denote by
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
to the transitive closure of
\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}}
. Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
is a union of disjoint total orders, so it is acyclic.

For describing the write-read relation, we distinguish between two cases: keys associated to variables in
\varphi
or to a transaction in
T
. On one hand, for every key
x_{i},1\leq i\leq m
, we define
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x_{i}}=\emptyset
. On the other hand, for every key
x
associated to a transaction
t_{x}
and every read event
r
in a transaction
t
, we impose that
(t_{x},r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
if
\mathtt{round}(t_{x})<\mathtt{round}(t)
while otherwise we declare that
(\textup{{{init}}},r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
. Then, we denote by
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}=\bigcup_{x\in\mathsf{Keys}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
as well as by
h_{\varphi}
to the tuple
h_{\varphi}={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
. A full depiction of
h_{\varphi}
can be found in Figure
0.B.1
.

We observe that imposing
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}=\emptyset
on every key
x\in\mathtt{var}(\varphi)
ensures that, for any witness of
h_{\varphi}
,
\overline{h}={({T,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},\overline{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}}})}
, if
(w,r)\in\overline{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}}
, then
\mathtt{WHERE}(r)(\mathtt{value}_{\overline{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}}}(w,x))=0
. In particular, this implies that each transaction
t_{i}^{j}
must read key
v_{i}^{j}
from a transaction that writes
1
as value while it also must read keys
v_{i}^{j+1\bmod 3}
and
v_{i}^{j+2\bmod 3}
from a transaction that writes
0
as value. Intuitively, this property shows that
\varphi
is well-encoded in
h_{\varphi}
.

\mathtt{INSERT}({\{x_{1}:1,1_{1}:-1\}})
\mathbf{1_{1}}
…
\mathtt{INSERT}({\{x_{k}:1,1_{k}:-1\}})
\mathbf{1_{k}}
…
\mathtt{INSERT}({\{x_{m}:1,1_{m}:-1\}})
\mathbf{1_{m}}

\mathtt{SELECT}(\lambda r:\mathtt{eq}(r,v_{1}^{0},v_{1}^{1},v_{1}^{2}))

\mathbf{t_{n}^{0}}

\mathbf{0_{1}}

\mathbf{t_{1}^{2}}
…

\mathtt{INSERT}({\{v_{n}^{2}:0,v_{n}^{0}:1,t_{n}^{2}\}})

\mathbf{t_{n}^{2}}
